r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
There is a grid of N^2 squares with N rows and N columns.
Let (i,j) denote the square at the i-th row from the top (1\leq i\leq N) and j-th column from the left (1\leq j\leq N).
Each square is either empty or has a piece placed on it.
There are M pieces placed on the grid, and the k-th (1\leq k\leq M) piece is placed on square (a_k,b_k).
You want to place your piece on an empty square in such a way that it cannot be captured by any of the existing pieces.
A piece placed on square (i,j) can capture pieces that satisfy any of the following conditions:

- Placed on square (i+2,j+1)
- Placed on square (i+1,j+2)
- Placed on square (i-1,j+2)
- Placed on square (i-2,j+1)
- Placed on square (i-2,j-1)
- Placed on square (i-1,j-2)
- Placed on square (i+1,j-2)
- Placed on square (i+2,j-1)

Here, conditions involving non-existent squares are considered to never be satisfied.
For example, a piece placed on square (4,4) can capture pieces placed on the squares shown in blue in the following figure:

How many squares can you place your piece on?

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N M
a_1 b_1
a_2 b_2
\vdots
a_M b_M

Output

Print the number of empty squares where you can place your piece without it being captured by any existing pieces.

Constraints


- 1\leq N\leq10^9
- 1\leq M\leq2\times10^5
- 1\leq a_k\leq N,1\leq b_k\leq N\ (1\leq k\leq M)
- (a_k,b_k)\neq(a_l,b_l)\ (1\leq k\lt l\leq M)
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

8 6
1 4
2 1
3 8
4 5
5 2
8 3

Sample Output 1

38

The existing pieces can capture pieces placed on the squares shown in blue in the following figure:

Therefore, you can place your piece on the remaining 38 squares.

Sample Input 2

1000000000 1
1 1

Sample Output 2

999999999999999997

Out of 10^{18} squares, only 3 squares cannot be used: squares (1,1), (2,3), and (3,2).
Note that the answer may be 2^{32} or greater.

Sample Input 3

20 10
1 4
7 11
7 15
8 10
11 6
12 5
13 1
15 2
20 10
20 15

Sample Output 3

338

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

import sys
def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    N, M = map(int, input().split())
    moves = ((2,1),(1,2),(-1,2),(-2,1),(-2,-1),(-1,-2),(1,-2),(2,-1))
    cells = []
    append = cells.append
    for _ in range(M):
        a,b = map(int,input().split())
        i = a-1; j = b-1
        # append occupied
        append(i*N + j)
        for di,dj in moves:
            ni = i+di; nj = j+dj
            if 0 <= ni < N and 0 <= nj < N:
                append(ni*N + nj)
    cells.sort()
    # count unique
    cnt=0
    prev=-1 # But key can be 0; use None? Since keys nonnegative. prev = -1 works because key>=0.
    for x in cells:
        if x != prev:
            cnt += 1
            prev = x
    print(N*N - cnt)
if __name__ == "__main__": main()

Let's write final code:

import sys

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    N, M = map(int, input().split())
    moves = ((2,1),(1,2),(-1,2),(-2,1),(-2,-1),(-1,-2),(1,-2),(2,-1))
    cells = []
    append = cells.append
    for _ in range(M):
        a, b = map(int, input().split())
        i = a - 1
        j = b - 1
        append(i * N + j)
        for di, dj in moves:
            ni = i + di
            nj = j + dj
            if 0 <= ni < N and 0 <= nj < N:
                append(ni * N + nj)
    cells.sort()
    unique = 0
    prev = -1
    for x in cells:
        if x != prev:
            unique += 1
            prev = x
    print(N * N - unique)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "M pieces placed" and "the k-th piece is placed on square (a_k,b_k)" but if a piece is at (a,b), and another
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    N, M = map(int, input().split())

    moves = (
        (2, 1), (1, 2), (-1, 2), (-2, 1),
        (-2, -1), (-1, -2), (1, -2), (2, -1)
    )

    cells = []
    append = cells.append

    for _ in range(M):
        a, b = map(int, input().split())
        i = a - 1
        j = b - 1

        append(i * N + j)

        for di, dj in moves:
            ni = i + di
            nj = j + dj
            if 0 <= ni < N and 0 <= nj < N:
                append(ni * N + nj)

    cells.sort()

    unique = 0
    prev = -1
    for x in cells:
        if x != prev:
            unique += 1
            prev = x

    print(N * N - unique)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
